Yes, I remember, Ponni! My husband said at the time that no one speaks Tamil as beautifully as these Dravida Munnetra Kazhagam people.

Hemalatha! Do you know there are people in this country willing to argue that even that is false...?

### Image 2

They may call it false out of party hostility, but could even they be unaware of the truth? I have heard many people say that it is through them that the stature of the Tamil language has risen.

Not bad, Hemalatha! Every now and then a good matter falls even upon your ears.

Get away, you rogue! Did you think I was merely a frog in a well? I too know the course the town is taking and the course the world is taking.

All right, all right! One must also know how the time is passing. I cannot stay any longer... He and I are going to see a play. May I leave...?

Are the two of you setting out together to see a play...? Hmm! This must be what they call childhood returning.

Why, Hemalatha! Do you know astrology? How did you know the title of the play I am going to see?

The title of the play you are going to see?

Yes. You said, “Is childhood returning?” That is precisely it, but they have rendered it into good Tamil and are staging it as *Regained Youth*.

Which company’s play is it?

Company? Where are theatre companies now? People kept running their own companies until their heads were stripped bare. Now and then they form groups of actors and stage plays. Even in that there are countless difficulties. My younger brother has formed such a theatre group.

Now you tell us... Is *Regained Youth* the play your brother is staging? All right, all right... Is he the hero?

No, no... He wrote the story and dialogue...

### Image 3

Bravo, bravo, Ponni! If only you had told me yesterday, I too would have come.

Never mind. This will be the first performance. I will see it and return; if the play is good, I will take you along... It is getting late. May I leave...?

5

Even if you did not come yourself, Ponni, I came intending to see you.

Come, come! Is it not my good fortune that the dust of your feet has fallen upon a poor woman’s hut?

What is this, Ponni? Has seeing a play made you speak this way, you rogue?

I did not see the play at all!

You said you were going to see it.

I could see it only if it took place! It was cancelled because of rain.

That is how it should be. You thought you could go and see it without inviting any of us. Did it happen? It serves you right!

Leave that aside. When the play is staged again, I will certainly take you.

Ponni! Do you know of an outrage?

How would I know about an outrage?

Yes, yes... If it is a wonder, you will know.

The whole world and town are marvelling—have you not heard? Earlier they sent puppies into the heavens; now human beings themselves have gone...

### Image 4

When I hear all this, Ponni, I truly am astonished. How was it possible to accomplish it all?

Scientific knowledge is the reason for everything. Such is the force of research there...

Was research never conducted in our country?

How could you ask that?

If research had been conducted, there ought to be signs of it. Are there? Show us. In whatever age the elders invented the little oil lamp and the standing lamp, we stopped with those... If people elsewhere too had said that was enough, would electricity have come...?

Looking at it, one must say that every wondrous object was invented in foreign countries...

But there is one thing: even in our country the newspapers report the discovery of certain kinds of wonders...

What is this wonder of our country, Ponni?

The wonder of a woman bearing twenty children—the wonder of a two-headed goat being born...

Get away, you mischievous woman! Is that what you mean? I thought perhaps someone had discovered a wonder like those found in other countries.

Our newspaper people have a desire. When each country reports one marvel after another, they desire to report something too. Last week they printed in the newspaper that a woman had suddenly become a man...

What is this, dear? It sounds bizarre! A woman became a man?

### Image 5

Yes... It is in the paper. Crowds went to find out how and why, but it was no use. They apparently locked the woman away.

Why did they lock her up? People went to see precisely because it was a wonder.

Yes, but perhaps it is a lie. They may have arranged for no one to see her out of fear that the fraud would be exposed.

It may be... It may be...

Whenever we hear that some wonder like this has occurred, the government should act at once. It should send eminent doctors to conduct an examination, investigate whether it truly is a wonder and what actually happened, and inform the public.

Yes... Why did the government not do that?

Why ask me that question, dear? Do I belong to the cabinet party...?

Do you know the news? A women’s conference was held in Trivandrum, and apparently went very well. Neela, the daughter of our jewellery-shop Narayana Chettiar, attended it... Ponni! Trivandrum has a “Living College”—the zoo—and they say it is very fine.

Goodness! Did Neela, who went to the women’s conference, return after seeing only tigers and bears everywhere?

It is your occupation to seize upon every little thing. If she went to a conference, could she not tour the town? Would she sit inside the conference from the moment she arrived until the moment she returned? Who could do that? People nowadays set out saying “great conference” or something of the kind precisely to tour a town! Have you noticed, Ponni? Many buses now leave expressly for pleasure trips. Only yesterday some relatives of my brother-in-law came to our house from Madurai...

### Image 6

They had apparently spent ten days seeing many towns.

Is that so...? Did Neela go and return in the same way?

Goodness, no! Neela went especially for the conference. Her elder sister works in some department for women, I hear. The salary is apparently three or four hundred.

They may even pay five hundred. If they are women from influential families, they receive whatever salary they ask. And if they are women from ministers’ households, the salary is still higher. They also receive expense money.

Neela’s elder sister is a widow, poor woman. Her husband died of typhoid. Poor thing, she has known no happiness.

Someone who speaks of so much reform should remarry, shouldn’t she?

How could she, Ponni? It is a great family; everyone in it is educated and holds a very high office.

Could even people so educated neither arrange the remarriage of Neela’s sister nor find the heart for it?

Would the relatives permit it? If she behaved that way, what man would come to marry the other women? You are utterly witless.

With things in such a state, why attend a women’s conference?

All the great leaders apparently came—the Governor himself attended.

Yes, I too was told. A minister called Morarji Desai attended. Do you know why he came? I do not know whether it is true, but a Third Five-Year Plan is coming, and apparently it will need a great deal of gold.

### Image 7

Did he come to Trivandrum to buy gold? Isn’t that city famous for things made of ivory?

He did not come to buy gold. It was a women’s conference, so many women would attend. If he observed now who wore how much jewellery, when the need arose they could seize it, couldn’t they? That is why he came.

What is this, Ponni—one of your own services?

That is how you will talk. I did not say this. The daughter of our Ekambaram of the auction shop told me.

How would she know?

She writes essays and stories for the paper. That is how she must know all this.

Very well. Have women entered newspaper work too?

Why are you so surprised? Do you know that the official next to the Collector of Chingleput District is apparently a woman?

Who is there to tell me all this, Ponni? Sometimes I myself become curious and ask my husband. Will he answer? Never.

It is your habit deliberately to make some baseless accusation against your husband. You are a wicked woman!

Get away, Ponni! The word “wicked” reminded me of that outrage.

Which outrage are you going to tell us about?

It is the matter of our Vatsala. Three days ago I thought, “Poor woman, her heart must be suffering terribly; let me at least go and say four words of comfort.” So I went. Vatsala has always been rather sharp-tongued. She moves with us, but she will not speak with an open heart.

### Image 8

Leave your description aside and tell us what happened.

I went and spoke to give her peace of mind, Ponni, but she snapped and snapped at me and wounded my own heart.

What did you say that made Vatsala angry?

You are just like her. Would I seek out her house in order to anger her?

Even if you did not do it deliberately, you may unknowingly have said something.

Yes, dear, I am eight years old and do not know how to speak. Go away.

What connection is there between age and speech? Does one’s skill in speaking increase as one grows older? The power of speech diminishes with age.

Ponni! There is apparently someone called Visvesvaraya who is now a hundred years old.

Yes. He is still doing well. Visvesvaraya was a great expert—the Mysore State.

So many people go to Mysore for Dasara. Who is there to take us? If I ask my husband, he says, “Tell your brother to take you.” If I ask my brother, he says, “Very well, let us see. Very well, let us see.”

Your brother seems the Kamaraj “type”—whatever anyone asks, he says, “Very well, let us see.”

Perhaps Kamaraj says “Very well, let us see” because saying it that way gives everyone a certain satisfaction.

### Image 9

Now people say something quite different. Everyone has begun to say that Kamaraj completes no task, merely passes the time saying “Very well, very well,” and that this is his trick. Wanting to satisfy everyone, Kamaraj began uttering the mantra “Very well, let us see”; now everyone has declared that his words have neither salt nor savour...

Has even that gone wrong now, Ponni? Wouldn’t the chief minister who rules a state understand this much?

How could he understand? It is not something understood by ruling a state; only those who rule a household understand it.

Enough riddles, Ponni. Explain clearly.

Now, whenever anyone asks Kamaraj for anything, he escapes by saying, “Very well, let us see.” If, like others, he had married and run a household, could he keep passing the time saying “Very well, let us see; very well, let us see” to everything his wife asked? Would she leave him alone?

Oh, is that what you mean, Ponni? No merit came from Neela’s visit. You should have attended the women’s conference in Trivandrum.

Who would invite us? Even if we had gone, our mouths would not have remained silent. I would have said: “Enough talk of women’s rights. We continue to live like an old almanac, without the least knowledge of the course the world is taking. Is that just? We still circle peepal and banyan trees. We read omens from lizards and cats. At the very mention of Rahu Kalam we tremble. Yet we wander from shop to shop demanding jewellery in the newest styles. One braid has become two! Instead of wearing a small bunch of jasmine or mullai, we have turned the whole head into a flower basket! Nor do we stop with applying black to our eyebrows...”

### Image 10

“We colour our lips and paint our nails. Apart from learning these things, have we acquired even a drop of new knowledge?” I would have spoken like that...

Would you have stopped there? Would you not also have denounced Brahmins?

Did you see that? Did you see? Even you flare up in anger at once...

May I come in...? You seem to be discussing some secret... I have arrived...

Who is it...? Ah, our Manonmani! Come, come! Ponni, do you know who she is? She studied with me when we were little...

Greetings... Come in... Sit down.

Manonmani! Her name is Ponni. Only the name is gentle! Her speech will be like a spark of fire. There is not the least deceit or guile in her. If I fail to see her even for one day, the day will not pass for me.

Is that so...? You have always been fortunate in everything...

You, of all people, must admire my fortune, Manonmani. Leave that aside; when did you arrive?

Do not be angry. It will soon be four days since I came.

Four days! Did you never remember that I live on this side? You are a hard-hearted creature—how else could you have entered the doctor’s profession? Ponni! Manonmani is a doctor, do you know?

That is a good thing. A doctor’s work benefits the town.

May I call you by your name?

Freely... What would be wrong in that?

I like your name very much...

The name Ponni, you mean? In our country, from ancient days, we have had the custom of calling people Ponnu, Thangam, Muthu, Maragatham, Manikkam, Kamalam, and seeking a pleasure in such names...

### Image 11

Did you hear that, Manonmani? This is Ponni’s way! Ask one question and she immediately begins speaking in a torrent...

There is nothing false or wrong in what she says... I like Ponni very much.

Then take Ponni along with you to Calcutta.

Do you live in Calcutta?

Yes, Ponni. It is nearly two years since I went there.

Manonmani is clever, do you know, Ponni? Do you know why she went to Calcutta...?

Enough of your mischief. Do not fabricate some rumour...

Ponni knows whether I tell rumours or not. Why are you trying to close my mouth? Take your hand away... Ponni! Her bridegroom lives there... She went there herself to ask when the marriage would be...

Would any woman ask that? You tell her, Ponni.

To ask need not mean speaking. She has eyes, doesn’t she, to tell a thousand stories.

And what about your own eyes? Are they so innocent? Pay no attention to what she says... Manonmani! The papers report riots in Calcutta and the surrounding places. Is it true?

What appeared in the papers is only a little. What happened made everyone tremble. Go away! Nothing but rioting, stabbing and disorder... For ten or fifteen days we were terrified even to walk in the street.

Mani! This Ponni here can sit in this very place and make one entire circuit of all the twice-seven, fourteen worlds.

### Image 12

Isn’t it necessary to know the affairs of one’s town and the affairs of the world?

All right, enough! Having learnt them, perhaps she is going to rule some country.

Why ask as though she should not rule, Mani? Do you know who now governs Ceylon...?

I know, dear, I know. A woman! But do you know what is happening? They say she imposes heavy taxes on all foreigners and treats them unjustly.

Leave justice and injustice aside. Must not each people build and protect their own country as they wish? What right have we to disparage that right?

Bravo, bravo! I arrived at exactly the right moment... It is I—Pattu...

Pattu indeed! The noise you made as you arrived sounded as though a whole string of firecrackers had been set alight. Why are you looking like that...? This is Manonmani. Residence: Calcutta. Profession: cutting and stitching.

What an elegant way to say she is a doctor...

Is it at a government hospital in Calcutta...?

No, no... A small private clinic of her own...

Manonmani, Pattu is studying to be a lawyer. Her mouth is already a weapon. Once she becomes a lawyer too, we are finished.

Let her speak, let her speak... One must obtain the whole deposition before cross-examining, mustn’t one? Keep quiet, Ponni!

Pattu, you must confine your agitation to the court, understand? If you display it to me, look—the bridegroom is coming. I will make him cut your tail close to the stump. Know that!

## Part 5

### Image 1

Oh, indeed...! You will say it, he will do it, and I will remain quiet... Is this some medieval age? There is law; if anyone violates it, even an emperor among emperors will be caught and have to count prison bars. You must understand that... How could you comprehend any of this? If someone asks you to prepare appalams and vadams, you will make basket after basket and hand them over...

Perhaps your evil eye has fallen upon them, for even appalams are in danger now...

What danger is there to appalams? They are sent even to Calcutta from here.

Hear this injustice, Mani! Should this government impose a tax even on appalams—a sales tax, and a heavy one at that...?

Why not try holding a procession crying, “Abolish the appalam tax completely!”

Pattu! You can only mock. Why speak merely of a procession? An entire conference has been held...

A conference for appalams...?

A conference to abolish the appalam tax. They even discussed it in the Legislative Assembly...

Discussed what? Appalams, vadams and all these things...?

Yes... What else is there to discuss? They discuss and decide atomic science and all that in Delhi! Our government must at least discuss appalams and vadams...

Just look at Ponni’s insolent tongue!

If I state what exists, is that an insolent tongue? In Calcutta even Congress leaders now speak in strong condemnation of Delhi’s course...

Very well! Have you too joined Ponni...? That settles it. No one will be able to restrain her now.

### Image 2

May a provincial government speak against or condemn the central government? The law would not permit it...

Pattu speaks law—she is studying to be a lawyer, after all.

Pattu! Bengal’s chief minister, Dr Roy, is extremely courageous. He fears no one. He often speaks in condemnation of Delhi’s course.

That must be wrong in law. How can he criticise the great government?

Go and ask him! He will throw the law you studied back into your face and send you away saying, “Go, go! Law exists for the people; people do not exist for the law!”

I know nothing about that. Everyone must act according to law. In the Nanavati matter, the court has said that even the order issued by the Governor was not valid in law...

Can even an act of the Governor be restrained...?

Governor or anyone else—Pattu will say that if an act is not lawful, one may file a “case” even against God...

One may. But the important point is how, where and upon whom the summons is to be served!

Madam! The master has arrived and says you must be brought out at once.

Go, dear Ponni. We too are leaving. We shall meet later...

Once a summons has arrived, Ponni is obliged to go.

This playfulness of yours, Pattu, will last only until you are married... Just see...

What connection is there between marriage and a change of disposition?

### Image 3

One cannot say there is absolutely no connection. Medical science itself contains evidence...

The master says she must go out and has asked me to bring her, madam.

All right, all right. Pattu, Mani, all of you leave. Let Ponni go attend to her affair...

Yes, the warrant has been issued... If she does not go even now, Ponni’s husband himself will come, arrest her and drag her away...

It looks as though Pattu will talk lawyer’s language and torment the life out of us until she goes to court and gets rebuked...

The answer to this will come next Friday. The court now adjourns...

6

Who is it? Ponni? Come in, dear! I wondered whether you too had listened to her words and stopped coming here. One cannot know anyone’s true nature while everything is green and prosperous, Ponni. Only when hardship comes does one learn who bears genuine love and what is merely an outward disguise. What is it, Ponni? Do you think I am a fool babbling whatever comes to mind, and therefore keep your mouth shut? If there is fault in what I say, tell me. I am not highly educated; I say only what strikes my heart. Ponni! When people who used to see us and speak with smiles before they learnt that the money we deposited in Palai Bank was gone now behave as though they have not seen us at all, how much pain the heart suffers! Will a time of hardship like this never come to any of them?

### Image 4

Will they always keep ruling with their banners flying?

Did you see that? Did you see? May noble women like you even think in their hearts that others must suffer the same hardship and loss that befell you? The mark of good women like you is surely to wish that no one else should suffer the pain that came to you...

It slipped from my mouth, Ponni! I am not such a cruel woman; you know that. The way some people behaved drove me to think and say all sorts of things.

In general, all of us have a great failing... That is the cause of this very confusion. We have little strength to bear grief, and when someone becomes distraught from hardship and loss, we do not know how to console or encourage them.

I don’t know, Ponni! You call it ignorance, but it does not seem that way to me. Now we are in pain—what do we need? There ought to have been feasts and celebrations. But I am a senseless woman, so I went to the function at Vadivu’s house...

That is good. The moment a hardship befalls us, our sole occupation must not be sitting in a corner and wailing.

That is what I thought when I went. But when I arrived, I did not at all like the look Vadivu gave me...

In the condition your mind is in, everything will seem hateful and strange.

No, Ponni! Certainly not. Do I not know even this much? Can I not tell whether people are happy to see us or are treating us with neglect...?

### Image 5

Very well, tell me. Vadivu looked at you in a peculiar way...

I thought we ought not to take notice of it and remained as I always do. But no—Vadivu said that I went and sat leaning against the pillar in the hall!

Yes. What is wrong in that, dear? I was tired from walking, and I have had a backache for four days besides! So I leaned against the pillar. What is wrong in that?

There is nothing wrong at all. Your backache made you sit that way. But Vadivu has that aunt of hers, you know...

That wretched widow...? What did she say...?

The moment I mentioned her, you called her a wretched widow. That alone shows that you and she do not get on...

What sort of woman is she...? If you ask how much torment I have suffered because of her, I shall tell you... She, indeed...!

Let her story wait. She saw you leaning against the pillar looking distressed. After seeing you, she apparently told Vadivu, “Go, poor thing, she is troubled at heart. Talk to her and cheer her up. If I speak, she may misunderstand something. You go and ask her to have some coffee.”

Who? She said that? Whom do you expect to believe this tall tale? The mere sight of me makes her face look as though sesame and horse gram are bursting upon it! Who would believe that she felt any tenderness towards me?

You will not believe it! I know. Your suspicion of her will not let you believe it. But I told you the truth just as I learnt it after making inquiries.

### Image 6

The moment she saw you, all her old hostility, anger and hatred flew apart like tufts of cotton. Vadivu did not know the details; that was all. Her aunt sent her to go and attend to you... Why hide the smile that comes of itself...?

Go on, Ponni! Does it not sound like a story? It is clear that she has coached you thoroughly and sent you here... Let me ask you this, Ponni. I shall accept what you say as true. Do you know what Vadivu asked me almost the moment she arrived? What is my situation now? What sort of hardship have I suffered? Without speaking even four words of sympathy about it, do you know what she asked me—in front of several people?

What did Vadivu ask?

She asked, “Auntie, your nose-stud is diamond, isn’t it?” The people nearby laughed. What an impudent tongue that girl has! Even though all our money was lost when the bank collapsed, ought she to ask me such a thing? “Your nose-stud is diamond, isn’t it?” Why should such a doubt arise? Does it not mean, “All your money is gone; how could you still be fit to wear a diamond nose-stud?” Does she not know that it is diamond? Whatever hard times may have come upon us, could we have become so destitute that we cannot wear even a diamond nose-stud?

Are you going to say more, or is that the end of it?

What sort of justice are you speaking, Ponni? Ought she to have spoken with such a barb?

She certainly ought not.

Especially when I am already heartsick...

Whatever the circumstances, it is wrong to speak barbed words that wound another’s heart. But you merely imagined that Vadivu spoke with a barb; she asked it quite naturally.

### Image 7

Have you come to make excuses for her? Was that natural conversation?

You foolish woman! You foolish woman! If you knew why she asked it, you would not keep flaring up. You would laugh until your stomach hurt. Vadivu had been pestering her father to buy her a diamond nose-stud. As she pointed out that this woman had one and that woman had one, she mentioned yours too. Vadivu’s father seems to be a formidable hand. To fool Vadivu he said, “You simpleton! You imagined that her nose-stud was diamond! It is not diamond; it is Rangoon kamalam.” She did not believe him. The silly girl insisted that it was indeed diamond. He declared emphatically, “Her husband has deceived her by saying it is diamond. It is only Rangoon kamalam.” That left her with a slight doubt. That is why she asked you—not to taunt or mock you.

Is that really so...? Vadivu is always a modest girl. She never talks rashly. I too wondered why she spoke that way on that day. Was that why she asked...?

That was the only reason. I speak only after having inquired into everything and learnt the truth.

You never reach any conclusion without a thorough inquiry, Ponni! Only after you told me has my mind become calm.

So your doubt is gone, is it not? Is there still any doubt? Vadivu spoke quite naturally...

But now a new doubt must have arisen in you...

How did you discover that, Ponni? You are a clever one! I truly do wonder whether this is really diamond, or whether my husband has deceived me just as Vadivu’s father said...

### Image 8

So the doubt has arisen after all! Do you know what you are going to do when he comes home...?

What will I do...? Stand and quarrel with him? That is not my way, dear...

It might be better if you did quarrel! You will pull a long face. You will speak in hints and insinuations. It is not our habit to ask directly and learn the truth, is it? “These days one cannot trust anyone’s word. If they find a gullible woman, what will they not do? They will pass off even Rangoon kamalam as diamond; they will polish brass and call it genuine gold.” Will you not begin that way, circle around the matter, and keep needling him...?

Go away, you rogue! Who would not feel like asking and finding out?

Did I say it was wrong to feel that way? One must ask directly and learn the truth. One must not speak in circles, leap from one thing to another, and increase both suspicion and strife, must one...?

Very well, dear! I will not even open my mouth and ask anything. Let it be diamond if it is diamond. Let it go hang if it is Rangoon stone. Why invite trouble by asking about it?

That is not right either. Would it be good not to ask? That thought will keep burrowing into your mind. Do you know all that it will do?

What will it do...? Tell me that too!

It will not let you wash your face and put on your forehead mark. It will not let you wear a fine sari. It will not let you laugh. Every pot and utensil will come crashing down. The tempering will sputter even without mustard seeds. Onion skins will float in the curry without being peeled. The mat will lie on one side and the pillow on the other. The milk will boil over into the stove. Hmph! Are not all these the methods by which we wage war!!

### Image 9

Ponni! Your very mistake was being born a woman!

He too says something like that now and then. Leave that aside. Tell me, was there anything wrong in what I said? Is this not what happens when women become angry?

Yes, yes—as though men create no commotion at all when they become angry!

Did I say that? Neither women nor men know the way to conduct themselves so that a family remains cheerful and free of disorder. It is a great art! People study all manner of arts, but no one seems to study the art of family life.

Why don’t you start a college for it, then!

Is it enough to make fun? Have the women who study in colleges mastered this art? Do you know something? An inquiry was conducted to discover what the women studying at Karachi University thought. It revealed one fact. Sixty-four women out of every hundred apparently said that they did not intend to marry at all—that they had no desire to do so...

What an outrage, dear! They do not want marriage at all?

Yes! They apparently said firmly that they did not.

Why such an aversion to marriage?

I do not think it is even aversion. If they marry, they must accept and manage the responsibilities of a family. These women have no desire to accept that responsibility. They may be afraid as to whether they possess that ability.

They are women in Pakistan, are they not?

Yes. What of it? If such an inquiry were conducted here, the women would give the same answer as those women did.

### Image 10

They say all sorts of restrictions have been imposed upon college women in Pakistan...

How did you come to know that? You are the woman who says, “Ponni, I am a frog in a well; what do I know of the world?” How did you learn this?

It is all what he read in the paper and told me. Tell me the details, dear...

I saw in the paper that at some colleges in Pakistan rules have been laid down forbidding women to wear nylon or lipstick, or to speak to men inside the college without cause. It seems women even have a separate entrance into the college.

Are there such restrictions, Ponni? Looked at one way, it seems that society does need some such restraints. Acting entirely as one pleases sometimes leads to indecency... Can one deny it? Must people walk hand in hand before they count as friends? Does respect mean that a man and woman must kiss each other on the cheek? Is this all that civilisation means? There must be some restraints.

Once there is clarity, there will be no indecency. But can that clarity arise quickly? One cannot say. Can everyone distinguish mere glitter from solid gold? Therefore, restraints must remain until maturity develops. But women must not be subdued and suppressed on that pretext.

That is right! But these days, in other countries, men and women embrace each other and caper about in the name of women’s freedom—we see it in the cinema. That ought not to happen.

It is true that today people blame it on civilisation, dance the “rock-and-roll,” and ruin themselves. But in former times...

### Image 11

...the gopis are said to have danced the “rasa lila” with Gopala Krishna in the name of devotion. Was that not wrong too...?

You will never give up your propaganda work, will you? It is as though you have been waiting for a chance to stick in the needle... You cannot sleep unless you preach the Self-Respect Party’s doctrine...

If I say this, will you simply dismiss it as the Self-Respect Party...?

I am not dismissing it, Ponni! I tell you truly: I argue against you only in jest. More and more, my heart feels that the Self-Respect principles you speak of are right.

Bless you, noble woman! If you have come that far, it is enough.

But I have not gained the courage, as you have, to cast the old ways off all at once without regard for reproach.

There is no want of courage. But there is a mistaken belief that honour lies only in following the old ways. That is what restrains people like you in your hearts.

Everything will set itself right as time passes.

Yes. Is the world as it was before?

All last month there was fear that a world war would break out, Ponni! How is it now?

There is still fear—fear that a world war may break out! That very fear is now our protection.

As poverty rises, prices are bound to rise too, from gold all the way down to bran. I too went and listened to a DMK meeting...

Well, well! Has talk of the DMK begun too...? Who spoke...?

### Image 12

Navalar—that is, Nedunchezhiyan. He was the one who said it. “To know whether this government is good or bad, one need only look in the kitchen. Why search for a thousand and eight reasons?”

What is there in the kitchen...?

Are the rice, dhal, chillies, ghee and oil in your house kept in the bedroom? They are in the kitchen, are they not? If you go into the kitchen, you understand how outrageously prices have risen!

Oh, did he say that?

Yes. Prices keep climbing so unjustly, and he asks whether a government that watches with folded arms can be called a good government.

Ponni! There—my husband is coming. He has had a cough for four days!!!

You knew it was “he” coming merely from the sound of the cough...? Very well... very well... I shall take my leave.

Ponni! From now on, all of us must be as we were before...

It is my habit always to remain the same. Shall I go...?

Do, Ponni! Tell Vadivu that I suspected her needlessly.

You too clear up your doubt—there he is. Ask him and find out whether it is diamond or not!

Go away, you rogue! Even if he lovingly bought me a piece of glass, would it not be equal to a genuine diamond, dear...?

All right, go! What will you gain by saying that to me...? Go and say it to him...

Now it is your back that is asking for a thump...

Only my back...? Hmph... Your cheek is asking for one.

## Part 6

### Image 1

7

Ponni! You said that if I began talking about the nose-stud, it would only end in needless trouble... That is exactly what happened... I ought to put sugar in your mouth, dear.

What is this—do you have sugar in such abundance that you can heap it into my mouth?

Are things as they were before? Sugar seems to be available a little more freely now. And they say new sugar mills are being started one after another in our country, do they not?

Yes, production will certainly be greater from now on. But it looks as though another difficulty may arise. The paper says that an enormous quantity of sugar is to be sent from our country to America.

Why are they sending it to America...?

For money, of course. Until now America had been buying sugar from the country called Cuba. Now there is hostility between Cuba and America...

Hostility—does that mean war has broken out? Tell me that, Ponni!

The two countries seem to be waging such a war of words that many people fear a real war may break out...

Yes, Ponni! We know that if war should break out, the whole world would be destroyed at one stroke. Knowing this, why must one country quarrel with another in this way?

That is precisely what all the thinkers who want justice and concord to prevail in the world keep pointing out...

### Image 2

What use is their saying it? Hostility keeps flaring!

But it is only because those great thinkers repeatedly offer such counsel that a great war capable of destroying the world has not occurred.

Whatever America’s reason for becoming hostile to Cuba, why should it refuse the sugar it had habitually bought?

Cuba earns revenue only by selling its sugar. America’s intention is to stop that revenue...

In other words, what they call striking a person in the stomach.

Yes! But would Cuba remain quiet? It has taken over the banks, hotels, factories, plantations and forests belonging to Americans there...

What happens to the money they invested?

Cuba will choose and pay whatever sum it pleases; America must accept that.

Just look at that courage!!!

Why should it not have courage? Khrushchev of Russia says, “If you lay a hand on Cuba, I shall not let you go unpunished.”

Now you are talking! They say that if one must seize hold, one should seize a sturdy tamarind branch. Cuba has acted just so...

Leave the Cuban quarrel aside! Tell me about your diamond nose-stud quarrel... Here comes Pattu, the lawyer...

It seems I have arrived at an opportune moment! I heard something about quarrels and quarrels...

Pattu! This is the kind of quarrel that gives you work. Is it not...? This is a dispute between husband and wife...

### Image 3

Good heavens! Do you know nothing of the matter? In America, half the lawyers earn fat incomes from precisely these quarrels...

But Ponni! How can one speak in court, without any embarrassment, about quarrels between husband and wife?

If one worries about embarrassment, one must simply lie there and endure the blows...

Pattu! Pattu is speaking the lawyer’s smooth language! Keeping it unspoken causes pain. As you say, one may have to lie there bearing the wounds. But if one goes to court, one’s nose will be cut off in disgrace...

It is by brooding over that very fear that many women surrender their rights and remain with tearful eyes.

Not bad! From now on, Pattu will charge no money at all for maintenance suits...

Who? Pattu? Good heavens! Pattu studied to become a lawyer precisely so that she could earn plenty of money...

Many people besides you think only of the large fees paid to lawyers. Your eyes do not see the plunder in trade.

When she says “trade,” Pattu does not mean the honest business that keeps proper accounts, avoids the black market, and duly pays every tax owed! Pattu must mean those traders who, for plunderous profit, do not shrink even from starving people to death. Is that not so, Pattu?

Yes! You must have seen in the paper that certain fellows clandestinely brought thirty or forty thousand rupees’ worth of camphor from Ceylon. They were apparently caught by the police.

### Image 4

Otherwise, thirty thousand rupees’ worth of camphor would have brought them three lakhs in profit.

Can that be called profit? Call it plunder.

It is plunder! But plunder committed in broad daylight! The government merely watches. Had it seized and dragged away even ten such men and punished them severely, at least the others would have felt some fear.

They say black-marketeers are punished terribly when caught in Pakistan...

Pakistan is apparently still under military rule.

Yes! No revolt, disorder, conspiracy or strife raises its head.

If it does raise its head, the military stands ready to stamp it out...

That is why Ayub Khan, who governs Pakistan, is touring foreign countries.

There was a meeting at which they said that Pandit Nehru had yielded far too much to Pakistan in the canal-water agreement...

That? Some people want enmity between Muslims and Hindus to persist forever. That is the talk of such people. But everyone who knows the matter has spoken with happiness that a dispute lasting many years has been resolved; they have praised the agreement...

There remains the Kashmir dispute. If a compromise could be reached in that unresolved matter too, there would be peace.

Not peace alone! The crores upon crores they pour into spending on the army would be saved.

### Image 5

That money could be put to some other useful and worthy purpose. Pattu! What is your opinion about this matter?

Where do you give me room to state my opinion? Whenever I begin one subject, each of you starts another new one.

Yes, Ponni! Pattu began what seemed an interesting subject, and it was we who became absorbed in digressions.

What I came to say was that women must not remain dumb creatures enduring cruelty, humiliation and pain. Did not the talk somehow turn to going to court in order to fight for one’s rights?

Yes! A major suit is now proceeding in a Paris court. You would all be astonished to hear of it. The ruler of the princely state called Kapurthala married a white woman...

Wait a moment, Pattu! Ponni! Are there no beauties in our country, that our men go abroad and return bringing a white woman with them? That is why I asked. I do not know, dear! White women do not seem beautiful to me.

What if they do seem beautiful to you? Are you a man, that you might marry one as the Maharaja of Kapurthala did? Listen to the rest. The Maharaja of Kapurthala died, and his white wife went abroad. Now she has filed a suit. Tell me what you think it is for...

It must be for maintenance!

Nonsense! Must her life depend on receiving maintenance money? She has filed suit claiming that jewellery worth one crore rupees belongs to her.

### Image 6

Jewellery worth one crore rupees?

Why! Are you thinking of how much I suffered to buy this diamond nose-stud that cost merely three or four hundred rupees?

No! Not that—go on! How much jewellery would there have to be for it to be worth a crore? Could there be a robe set with diamonds? Even if it were diamond, could it be worth one crore rupees?

What is astonishing in that? A new diamond has now been found. They say no price can even be fixed for it—it is said to be that valuable.

Where did they discover this diamond?

In Africa. It apparently weighs 184 carats. An African labourer found it in a mine’s refuse heap. They gave him a reward of ten thousand rupees.

Just look at his good fortune! He need never work as a labourer again. He has received ten thousand!

Leave his good fortune aside; notice his honesty. He found a priceless diamond. The wicked thought of secretly keeping it did not arise in him. He voluntarily handed it to the owner of the mine. Is that not honesty!

I wonder who will buy that diamond. They will have to pay no small sum—who knows how many lakhs they will ask?

Why are you sighing now? Is that a matter for people like us to ponder? Why do you have such a desire?

It is only natural for desire to arise... Are we Vinoba, able to cast desire away and become renunciants? For that matter, even he has an immense desire: the desire to obtain land as a gift from those who possess too much and give it to the landless! No one can destroy desire.

### Image 7

Why can it not be done? Are not ascetics those who have subdued every desire?

Ascetics, indeed! “Ascetic” is the name given to those who have subdued desire. Is that what you are saying?

What is this, Ponni dear! Are you disputing even the matter of ascetics?

Why would I dispute it? I merely laughed when you said that ascetics were those who had subdued desire. Do you know why? The story of Vishvamitra came to mind.

Pattu! Ponni will not stop talking now! Her Self-Respect propaganda will begin. Hmph! Let your propaganda begin! “All who wear saffron are lechers!! They must never be trusted...”

Ponni, can one say that without qualification? She says Tamil literature is her very life. If so, she ought to know Ilango Adigal...

Those who study Tamil literature know Adigals, the virtuous, the wise and the learned; but they do not know the *tavasi*! Why? Because Tamil Nadu has had *thuravis*, renunciants, but never *tavasis*, ascetics...

Pattu! You have studied law. Tell me, could even you argue this way, claiming that every word has a different meaning...?

I am not arguing. I say that Tamil Nadu has had renunciants, but no ascetics.

Are a renunciant and an ascetic different?

Yes! One who has renounced all the pleasures and desires of life is a *thuravi*, a renunciant. A renunciant will also serve the people. A *tavasi* is not like that. He will beg, dance about and prostrate himself, asking to be granted salvation. He will starve himself and, heedless of cold or sun, lie chanting “Om Namah Shivaya” or “Vitthala! Vitthala!”

### Image 8

Ilango Adigal was a renunciant! It was he who composed the magnificent epic *Silappathikaram*, which has won immortal fame...

Ponni asks, “What book did Vishvamitra write? What literature exists in his name?”

The responsibility for answering that too belongs to Ponni.

Me! If I say anything, you will immediately seize the occasion to make mischief: “Ponni is preaching Self-Respect propaganda!” Hmph! Go on—what hardship or loss does that cause me? The renunciant Ilango Adigal gave the world *Silappathikaram*; Vishvamitra gave it Shakuntala. Was he not an ascetic? That is why!!!

Go away, you mischievous woman! Are there no ascetics who have avoided improper conduct?

Did I say that? No! The definition of *tavasi* is one thing; the meaning of *thuravi*, renunciant, is another.

Pattu! This is how Ponni is. Saffron robes and water-pots, sacred ash, temple cars and festivals—she will pour out fierce denunciations of them all, rapidly and vigorously...

Her husband has spoiled Ponni by indulging her so much.

Do you mean Ponni’s husband? Good heavens! Once he was an ardent Saivite. Later he joined the Self-Respect Movement. That is why so many people now wait upon him and do his bidding...

Yes, does Ponni even travel with her husband to other towns to conduct propaganda...?

Did you not know? When he organises a party conference, you ought to see Ponni then!

It seems she must deliver speech after speech at the conference!

### Image 9

She does not merely speak. She attends to every task at the conference. From collecting funds to looking after the diners, it is Ponni who does everything!

Is that so! Then say that Ponni has a great deal of influence in their party.

Enough of the mockery! Pattu! Whether it is conference work or any other work, can I sit idle and watch while he works? Must I not work alongside him...?

Yes, yes! Only when one works like that will the whole world praise one. Even now, the paper says that women did most of the election campaigning for Kennedy, who has become President of America.

That may be. But, Pattu, Kennedy is now President of America, and I do not remember his wife going anywhere to speak or ask for votes.

Kennedy’s wife was in the final stage of pregnancy! The baby is due next month!! Could she travel from town to town doing election work in that condition? That is why she did not campaign. The paper says that the wife of Nixon, who contested against Kennedy and lost, joined her husband in the election work... Her toil bore no fruit. When she heard that her husband had lost, she apparently sobbed and sobbed...

Alas, poor woman! It must indeed have caused her anguish. But what can be done? Once there is an election, one person will win and the other will lose! That is the rule.

That is the rule, but how can grief fail to pierce one when defeat becomes known?

Victory and defeat are natural in an election. Would anyone become distressed over that?

### Image 10

What does it matter to you, dear? You will speak freely—you do not worry at all about victory or defeat.

Do you know Navalar Nedunchezhiyan of our organisation? He will not call defeat “defeat” at all. He calls the defeated “those who lost the opportunity for victory.”

Just look at that amusement...!

Amusement? Nonsense! Navalar Nedunchezhiyan believes there should be no empty pomp or hollow bravado. If he began explaining the meaning of “one who lost the opportunity for victory,” he would speak lucidly for at least half an hour...

Ponni, they say women outnumber men in America, and that the strength of the vote rests with women...?

Is that so? Apparently the position is similar in regions such as Pondicherry and Karaikal.

Is numerical strength alone enough?

It is not! But without even that, there would be still more trouble, would there not? It is only because men know that women are numerous and possess the vote that they argue for women’s rights, at least for appearance’s sake...

No advancement will come merely from continually demanding “rights, rights.” The moment we demand our rights, the thought of our duties must rise uppermost in us.

Yes—but “duty” does not mean the saying, “Though he be stone, he is one’s husband; though he be grass, he is one’s man.” A woman must conduct herself with an understanding of her husband’s nature, but she must not remain enslaved. In our movement we call this a life-partner agreement.

Listen to that, Pattu! However many law books you read, you cannot learn it. Merely speak with Ponni and you can learn everything...

### Image 11

Do you all know something? It might even be called a secret...

Tell us, dear... Cut down the preamble. Save all your elaborate argument for when you speak in court tomorrow—you will be paid for it. Now tell us the matter briefly.

Must you speak this much merely to stop Pattu from enlarging upon a matter? You tell us, Pattu! Only when it is explained can the matter be understood; that is why Pattu speaks that way... Tell us, Pattu! What is the secret?

Our Nehru, you know...

What is this “our Nehru,” dear? You claim kinship as though you were acquainted with him...

I have spoken with him... in POONA.

When did you go to Poona? When were you there?

Goodness! The moment I said POONA, did you imagine that I had gone to the town called Poona? What I said was P-O-O-N-A in English...

Is that not the name of a town?

It is not a town’s name. It is the name of an organisation.

Oh! Is it like those things they call NATO and SEATO?

What is this, Ponni dear! Pattu says POONA, and you go on piling up NATO and PATO.

I have not gone anywhere... I am right here. Now when one says UN, you know and understand it, do you not...?

Yes! It appears in the paper every other moment... It means the United Nations Organisation...

Just as United Nations Organisation is shortened to UN, NATO and SEATO...

### Image 12

...and this POONA that our Pattu mentions must be the same sort of thing. Is that not so, Pattu?

Correct. POONA—“Peoples’ Organisation Orbit New Asia”—that is, the people’s forum in New Asia: POONA!

Enough, dear! My head spins from your POONA and the interpretation you give it. Leave that aside. Where did Nehru come into this POONA?

You foolish woman! Why would Nehru come here? Once at POONA we staged a little play. My college teacher played Kamala Nehru! I was Indira Gandhi!! I was jokingly referring to what I said then!

A fine joke, with your POONA and Nana... Do you know there is a book called *Nana*...?

It was written by Émile Zola, was it not!

Not bad—Ponni knows Émile Zola... Yes, why are you hanging your head? Are you ashamed that we have not read all this and must sit like dumb creatures while Pattu and Ponni converse...?

Oh, hush and keep quiet! You are the one with great learning! The moment Indira Gandhi’s name was mentioned, my heart grew troubled. Poor woman! Did you see what anguish came upon her...?

Yes, poor woman! Feroze Gandhi’s death was indeed a heavy blow to Indira, poor thing.

How could she bear it? Did not the whole country celebrate that Feroze Gandhi and Indira had made a love marriage...?

Not only a love marriage—an inter-community marriage...

Yes! They had everything needed to live happily...

## Part 7

### Image 1

A husband who filled her eyes, a life filled with love—is that a sorrow one can simply wipe away? What is she doing now...?

Indira is touring Mexico, is she not?

She is not there now, Pattu! She left Mexico and came to New York. The news says she watched the election that took place there...

Ponni! Whom are you talking about—someone who went to Mexico and came to New York...?

Indira Gandhi, of course.

What! It has not even been a year since her husband died... and already...?

A year! Indira Gandhi left for Mexico after merely a month!

What an outrage, dear! She had lost her husband without warning. They said he died suddenly of a heart attack...

Yes—she cried, she wept, she sobbed. Could she keep doing that alone forever?

But does that mean bearing even the grief of her husband’s death and going to America? What life-or-death business was there, what calamity demanded such haste...?

It concerns people in high places—that is how it will be. Her husband is gone. Whether she laments or rolls upon the ground and weeps, will the dead man return...?

I know that, dear... Even children in a veranda school know it!! But her turmeric is gone, her vermilion is gone, her flowers are gone, her *thali* is gone. Merely thinking of it makes us want to embrace her and weep. Indira Gandhi lost her husband without warning and then went to America—fie! To me it does not seem human at all. Whatever thousand reasons may be offered, I am prepared to mount any platform and say that what Indira...

### Image 2

...Gandhi did was wrong, wrong...

Pattu! Did you see her courage? Indira Gandhi is the Prime Minister’s only daughter! Yet she has become bold enough to mount a platform and condemn Indira’s conduct...

Why should one tremble to speak what is right? Even if I saw Indira Gandhi in person, I would ask her. What have I to fear? “Is this right, madam? Within a month of your husband’s death, could you harden your heart like this and go to America? Your father often speaks of Indian culture, Indian culture—I have seen it in the papers. Is this that culture?” I would ask her. What have I to fear?

We can consider speaking to Indira Gandhi later. Come and speak on this very subject one day at our POONA! Let Ponni preside...

You are asking me to come and speak, are you not? Where do I have time for all this, dear? Am I like you? Work lies piled up in mountains...

Why are you telling a baseless lie...?

Pattu! I accept the latter half of what you said and reject the former. What I said was a lie, but not one without a reason!

Why, Ponni! Why have you too joined that chatterbox lawyer girl and begun talking mischief and nonsense?

There is neither mischief nor nonsense. I know where you are going after telling us to get up and leave now.

Do you know astrology too?

If I knew astrology, would I not tell you myself whether you will get your Palai Bank money back or not? Pattu, get up! Get up! They are about to go to the Malayalam Magical Astrological Institute. There is the sound of the car. They are coming. We shall take our leave.

### Image 3

8

Ponni! Listening and listening to you, I too became enchanted and believed that women must have freedom and equality because that is how women live in civilised countries, and that we too ought to live that way... But, Ponni! The moment he told me that news, I understood what obscenities and outrages occur if women are given rights. No, dear—I want none of the women’s rights and equality found in foreign countries...

Have you finished, or is anything still left? The way you burst out like a buried firework shows that your anger has risen to your head.

I am not angry, Ponni! I tell you truly. Shame is eating me alive. However much women may wish to be equal to men, could they not confine themselves to showing it in their bearing and dress? Must they cast off every restraint and behave like a horde of savages...?

Who behaved savagely? Where, and what happened? If you tell us before pouring out your anger, we can understand the matter. It will also be easier to explain it...

Are you going to offer an explanation too—an explanation!

If you do not like the word “explanation,” leave it. Say instead that I shall bring the hidden truth to light...

You are going to bring the hidden truth to light! Do not make my stomach burn. As though it were hidden...!

What are you saying?

### Image 4

What are we saying? I am not asking you to do anything difficult. We ask you to cover your bodies with cloth and preserve modesty, dignity and human decency.

Who is now wandering about with her hair loose and dishevelled? Why are you speaking in such agitation...?

Why, Ponni! Tell me, what is the difference between a human being and an animal...?

How can I answer when you ask like that...? There are ever so many differences.

The chief difference between an animal and a human being is that a human fears the loss of modesty and acts accordingly...

Yes... Who has now told women to behave without modesty...?

Who? It is all that talk of yours about equal rights and civilisation.

I do not understand at all...

Little child! She knows nothing. Why, dear, you probe out things happening in every far corner of the world and tell them as story after story. Do you not know this? The shameless obscene dance being performed in the name of civilisation.

Do you mean the dance they speak of now, wearing dresses sewn from nylon cloth that do not properly cover the body...?

Not even that horror, dear. People who know morality denounce even dancing without properly covering the body as a devil’s dance... This is something worse, dear. They apparently bathe in sunlight—naked...

Oh! Is that what you mean? That is what makes you so angry.

### Image 5

If I caught those wretched women before me, I would thrash them to pieces and then attend to everything else. Donkeys, horses, dogs, jackals, wolves, camels, goats and cattle go about as they were born... Are human beings to become the same? And women, above all! Our elders have said that modesty is a woman’s ornament. Yet these creatures apparently wear no cloth at all... They lie and roll about naked in the sunlight. There is supposedly some health in it—accursed health! There is an association for this, it seems! And that association has a woman president! Ponni! Such vile creatures ought to be whipped...

In which newspaper did you see this?

This abomination...? Someone apparently told her about it...

Sunlight is commonplace in our country. In cold countries, where snow keeps falling, there is no sunlight at all. Whenever there is good sunlight, the people of those countries celebrate...

Why, Ponni! Could they not celebrate it with restraint and decorum...?

Am I arguing that what they do is right? Medical science itself says that certain illnesses disappear only when the sun’s rays fall upon the body...

Of course it does, dear, of course! “Wear no clothes... Lie rolling about in the sunshine and your body will turn to gold,” says the medical book she has read... A great doctor, this Manonmani...

Good heavens! Ask Dr Manonmani herself...

Ask her what? Shall we ask Dr Manonmani whether she knows those noble ladies who wander about naked... Manonmani, may you live a hundred years! Listen to this...

### Image 6

Manonmani! I shall explain it clearly...

You need neither explain nor confuse anything... I shall ask Manonmani. Why, dear, you studied to become a doctor, did you not?

Not only did I study; I am now working as a doctor, am I not?

Yes, yes—who denied that now? Does the book you studied say that if one lies with not even a cloth wrapped around the body, letting sunlight fall upon it, there will be no illness and the body will turn to gold...? No...? I ask only because I do not know. Tell me...

That is, Manonmani, someone has spread a sensational tale about a nudist association... That is why she is so enraged...

Is it wrong to be enraged? Must one agree that their going without clothes is an entirely proper act? Why, doctor dear! Do you too say it is right...?

Even I have not said it is right...

I say this as a doctor: it is sheer madness to think that whatever benefit they expect from sunlight can be obtained only here through an obscene and repulsive method like theirs.

Call it wickedness! An association, indeed, for such a deed! Would one not spit upon these shameless creatures...?

Would the association be in a place where many people can see them?

Civilisation has supposedly reached its peak... Civilisation! Is the destruction of reason called civilisation? Is disregarding modesty called civilisation?

### Image 7

Who said so? Civilisation is one thing; conduct of this kind is another... How many people do you think take part in this practice?

They say this association in England has five thousand members...

Very well. What is the population of England? These association members could not amount even to one in ten thousand. Does that not show that, apart from rash-minded people, the overwhelming majority in that very country will have nothing to do with it...? This is madness—madness adopted in the name of civilisation...

That is why, Ponni, I even feel disgust for this civilisation you speak of... Is it not through endless talk of civilisation that at least a few people commit this obscene act...?

Do obscene and repulsive things occur only in the name of civilisation? They occur in the name of religion too...

What new bear of a tale are you unleashing now, dear...?

Me? Is that my sole occupation? I am telling you what happens... There is a town called Periyapalayam...

Oh! Are you going to speak of the neem-leaf garment?

Yes. I saw it myself when I was a little girl...

People make a vow to the goddess of Periyapalayam: “I shall come wearing a neem-leaf garment and circle the temple.” This means that women must wear no cloth at all. Neem leaves are threaded together and given to them. That alone must be wrapped around the body...

Do all who visit that temple do this...?

No, no. Only those who have made that particular vow. Who is it...? Pattu? Come! Come! I am speaking about Periyapalayam.

### Image 8

A *palayam* is neither a city nor a village. Is that not so?

It is a sacred place with a goddess’s temple.

Is that so...? I was thinking of the age of the *palayakkarars*. In the days of the *palayakkarars*, what they decreed was law. There was no trial of a case. If they caught a wrongdoer, they apparently tied him to a tamarind tree and beat him.

Pattu! We can hear your legal question later. You tell us, Ponni! Pattu! This is a matter of a neem tree, not a tamarind tree...

We speak of the neem tree with such indifference. Every part of that tree is medicine—rare medicine.

Yes, they call the neem tree a *sanjeevi*, do they not! No illness will approach, and no evil spirit will touch one...

What is that? Is there a black wind within the wind...?

Go away, you foolish woman! Our elders gave the name “wind and blackness” to ghosts and demons...

Neem, mahua, pongamia, portia... How very many trees there are in our country. Medicines can be prepared from them to cure many diseases.

Not trees alone... There are herbs in abundance.

They say there is a mountain somewhere in the south devoted entirely to medicinal herbs...

There is a green leaf in our country capable of curing an incurable pain of the eye. They say it will be presented to Queen Elizabeth of England when she comes here...

Our country’s herbs can be classified and medicines prepared from them by sound methods. Did you see in the paper that Karumuttu Thiagaraja Chettiar of Madurai has said a research committee should work on this?

### Image 9

Yes, I saw it. It is a good idea...

All right, but they say he is a mill owner. What does he know about medicine, herbs and all this?

He spoke only after learning about it. Moreover, Pattu, if we study our Tamil literature properly and eagerly, we can gain a clear knowledge even of herbs and medicinal leaves. Karumuttu has a keen love of Tamil.

Now you are talking. It seems he must have gained information about herbs while studying Tamil literature.

About herbs alone? Some of the poets themselves were physicians. One poet bore the name Maruthuvan Damodaranar...

Today we do not even know the poets’ names... We forget them as soon as we finish studying them...

All right, but what is this? Ponni has forgotten the neem tree and turned elsewhere!

Yes... The mention of herbs brought Karumuttu to mind. He runs many educational institutions in Madurai—many different kinds of colleges. The only thing remaining is for him to found and run an agricultural college.

All right. Finish what you came to say.

Some people make a vow to the goddess of Periyapalayam and circle the temple wearing no cloth, but with threaded neem leaves tied around them. Some people blame it upon civilisation and run a nudist association. This disgrace occurs in the name of devotion.

Ponni! One question. You must answer clearly. Are there cattle in the Periyapalayam temple or near it?

### Image 10

Pattu! What sort of question is this? Would there be a town without cattle? There is even the proverb “a temple bull”...

Then no evidence is needed to prove that cattle are present. Ponni! A case has arisen—not a case that has arisen, but one that might arise!!! Shall I tell you?

When you say “case,” are you conducting one now?

Now? Nonsense! No! It is an imaginary case. Kannamma has made a vow to the goddess of Periyapalayam and is circling the temple wearing a neem-leaf garment.

Who is this Kannamma, dear...?

My client... That is, I am inventing the case, am I not? She is the principal figure in it.

That is, in your imaginary case, Kannamma is the accused... Is that it...?

Not at all! The accused are others. The principal bull and the temple trustee must not interrupt while I construct the case...

Are cases ever falsely constructed? May one construct them so?

One must not—but it does happen. Kannamma is circling the temple reverently, wearing a neem-leaf garment. A large new motorcar approaches, its horn blaring loudly.

Whose motorcar arrived?

It must belong to some other devotee.

No, no. Listen to me. Why must you construct the case wrongly on your own...? Can one construct a case without knowing the law...? Very well. At the sound of the motorcar, the bull becomes frightened. The temple bull moves towards Kannamma. She is circling the temple reverently in her neem-leaf garment. Can the animal understand that this is worship and a vow?

### Image 11

The neem-leaf garment gleams before its eyes! At once the bull seizes and tugs at the neem leaves Kannamma is wearing, intending to eat them. Many people see it and laugh. The man in the motorcar grimaces. Disgusted, he leaves the place.

Why so much detail about him, Pattu?

Would I mention it without reason? The man who came in the motorcar was Minor Masi; he was the match they had chosen for Kannamma. Do you understand the position? The woman who was to become his wife is being chased by a bull; she has no clothing, only leaves! Many people see it and laugh mockingly. Minor Masi thinks deeply. Considering it dishonourable to marry a woman who has become the object of so many people’s ridicule, he forgets the marriage proposal entirely. We have now reached the stage of filing a defamation suit! I am the one bringing the suit.

Wait, dear, wait! Against whom are you going to bring the suit?

Why, against the temple trustee, of course!

What did he do that you should sue him?

Not for what he did! For what he failed to do. Is not failing to do what ought to have been done also an offence? Kannamma was about to marry into a prominent family. When the prospective bridegroom saw her placed in a position where many people mocked her, he refused to accept her. How did she come to be ridiculed? The bull tugged at the neem leaves she wore in place of clothing in order to eat them. She was left in a disgraceful state, and many clapped their hands and laughed. Had the bull not done so, Kannamma would never have suffered this loss of honour.

Yes! That is right. But ought the defamation suit not be filed against the bull? What can the trustee do? What is his offence?

### Image 12

The trustee knows the temple’s forms of worship, including the practice of wrapping neem leaves around oneself as clothing.

Yes, he knows... Why, Ponni! Why are you listening open-mouthed as Pattu speaks? It was the bull that committed the offence, was it not? Say something...

Even as Pattu set out the case, it became clear whom she was going to name as the culprit. The trustee knows that a bull eats leaves. He also knows that women circle the temple wrapped in leaves. Does the trustee not know that a bull will spring at the sight of leaves? Therefore, his allowing the cattle to wander there was the cause of Kannamma’s loss of honour. The trustee is consequently the culprit! Pattu! Is that not it?

A flawless argument. Ponni! Instead of wasting your time preaching Self-Respect propaganda, become a lawyer. The whole town will be in an uproar—you will become a great personage.

Is there a law under which a human can be sued for something a bull did?

People have sued house owners for something a house did. Three men have been arrested in Bombay.

What offence did the house commit?

It collapsed. Five people died, twenty were injured, and six hundred lost their homes and are wandering the streets.

Was it the house in which they lived?

Yes! A five-storey building in Bombay—a building of the kind constructed solely to collect rent.

All right. Did the owner himself knock down the house because he wanted to kill these people?

## Part 8

### Image 1

Only a madman would do that, would he not? This man was no madman; he was a rich man. He kept collecting the rent and accumulating money, but paid not the slightest attention to the building’s dilapidated condition. He did not repair it.

Did the tenants not tell the landlord that the building was dilapidated...?

Were they alone in telling him? The Bombay Corporation itself issued notice that the house must be repaired immediately or there would be danger.

The landlord paid no attention at all...

Yes! The house collapsed. Five people died. Among them was even a fifteen-year-old girl, poor thing! Deliberately keeping a building in a dangerous condition, renting it out and making money, and maintaining the house in a state that endangered its residents and could collapse at any moment was a grave offence! Just see—the case is going to be tried!

Listen, Ponni dear, to Pattu’s skill in argument... Whether the court accepts it or not, we must now accept that the trustee may be sued for the bull’s offence.

Pattu invented that case. But is not the true culprit our own foolishness? Losing our reason in the name of religion was the first offence! Against whom should we bring suit for this? Pattu, does your law book say anything about it?

It says nothing about this. But when Self-Respecters like you conduct propaganda—if you demean religion, speak in a way that creates religious animosity, or speak in a manner that creates hostility between one caste and another—it contains plenty about all the sections under which a case may be brought...

### Image 2

Of course it does. Was not law itself created to preserve every ancient arrangement just as it is and to protect the rights of those who possess property?

Property and comforts, indeed! When they die, will they bundle their property and carry it upon their heads?

In ancient times, the kings of Egypt customarily accumulated property and took it with them when they died...

Ponni! What marvel is this?

Is that the only marvel? Those kings would build great tombs for themselves, with halls, upper storeys, inner chambers, tunnels and all the rest...

Build their tombs while still alive?

Yes! What assurance was there that others would build them splendidly after their deaths? So they spent great sums and built their tombs. They heaped within them the costly ornaments and jewels they acquired.

All these things inside the tomb?

Yes! Various kinds of beds, clothes, arrangements for bathing, dining plates, ivory objects, golden bedsteads and perfumes. They placed all of these in the burial chamber. When the king died, they carried the body into it and laid it there...

Would they then bring out the wealth in the burial chamber?

They would not even touch it; that would be a sin. The dead man would rise again and go to the upper world to live a second life! He would then need possessions suited to his rank... That is why.

### Image 3

If so, Ponni, enormous wealth must remain in these burial chambers even now.

Yes! That is why even now they are excavating one burial chamber in the expectation that it holds wealth. Once before, a great treasure was found.

Treasures like that are not found very often in our country, are they...?

Treasures are found! They will not be in tombs. Moreover, our country long ago condemned those vile mortals who toil to earn money only to bury and hoard it.

Do you know what the law says when treasure is found...?

What do we know? Are we lawyers like you?

As soon as a treasure is found, the government must be informed. Otherwise it is an offence. The treasure belongs to the government.

Then the government itself ought to dig and discover whether there is treasure, and what things—gold, iron, coal and so forth—can be found.

Hmph! Hmph! Our government, is it not! It digs plenty of pits—to plant flagpoles!!!

Go away, you mischievous woman! Do they not keep saying constantly that they are going to drill and see whether even petroleum can be found?

They do keep saying it! But they have done nothing yet.

What do I know of all this, dear? Is everyone a frog in a well like me...? You people know a great deal about affairs beyond the village.

Do you know the news about frogs?

### Image 4

They have robbed me of sleep at night with all their croaking. Do not tell me about that.

I do not mean that. They say frogs are much in demand.

Earlier they used to say monkeys were in demand.

Yes! Now it is the frog. A frog is apparently a very tasty dish to them.

Fie! My mouth is turning with nausea. Please be quiet.

Your mouth is turning with nausea! Doctor! Mano! What is the matter? Examine her and tell us...

Fie, you chatterboxes! Get up and run away. Are you making fun of me?

Why would I have another one at this age, dear? I already have four like golden idols...

9

The moment I heard, I began trembling violently, Ponni! The milk-woman said that the entire rear portion had collapsed with a crash, and I panicked. I was terrified, wondering what might have happened, and frantic to come at once. But the rain had to stop, did it not? The moment it eased a little, I came running.

I knew you would be frightened! When the rear wall collapsed, you were the one I thought of.

No one is hurt, are they? Tell me that first. Where were you then?

If I had been hurt, would I not be in the hospital? Fortunately, no one was harmed. I was in the front portion. Even as the rain poured and poured, I knew that the rear portion would not withstand it. So I did not so much as show my head on that side.

### Image 5

Where was he? At home, was he not?

No. He was distributing packets of food elsewhere.

How did he have the heart to leave you alone and go?

I was the one who told him to go. At least we live in a stone building. Was the suffering this rain caused to people in huts a small thing? We ought to help them as much as possible, should we not? I told him to go and attend to that work.

You are indeed stout-hearted. The whole town is terrified... Yet you remain alone after sending him out too.

I did not wish to remain at home either. I wanted to go with him and offer whatever help I could. He stopped me, saying, “Ponni! What could you do by coming? In some places the water is waist-deep, they say! It is one vast flood. If I take you along, my only work will be looking after you.”

He did the right thing. Suppose you too had gone somewhere, stumbled and fallen.

You would weep for four days saying that Ponni was gone. Would you give up your own life along with mine?

Go away, you rogue! Who would do that?

That is what you would say. But do you know? When the father saw that his daughter was in danger, he fainted and suffered a heart attack!

It seems his very life rested upon his daughter.

Yes. That is why, when his daughter was seized by dangerous pain, he cried out, “Alas! Oh!” A heart attack struck him and he writhed... The moment she saw it, the daughter screamed...

### Image 6

...“Alas! Father!” and collapsed. Both of them died...

Good heavens! What injustice is this, Ponni! Must such a thunderbolt strike one family?

The whole town shed tears when it heard that Somu of Jupiter Pictures and his daughter Shantha had died at the same time.

Merely hearing it pierces one with grief, Ponni! How will that family bear it?

It was a family that gave livelihoods to ever so many families, and such a fate befell it. Even if someone lies ill for a month or two, many doctors attend him and say survival is difficult, and he lingers, wheezes, coughs and dies, the grief cannot be contained. But this death—merely thinking of it feels like fire being poured into the heart... Hmph!

What can be done? A heart attack is like that. It takes only a few seconds. Life departs...

Ponni! These days the news that someone has died of a heart attack seems to come more and more often.

Yes, yes! Even Somu was apparently speaking normally at one in the afternoon. For just ten minutes he writhed, saying his heart was gripping him, and his life was gone.

They boast, “We have learnt to measure the cosmos; we have split the atom!” They say we are going to the sphere of the moon. They show pictures and say, “Look at the sphere of Mars.” Yet we cannot discover one good medicine to stop a heart attack. Every day they learn new arts only to destroy what exists... They call this a marvellous age! They say it displays wonder after wonder! What use is it? I do not feel that this age possesses any true greatness, Ponni.

### Image 7

Can one dismiss it so completely? Destruction, danger, disease and death will exist in every age. Even in this age, proper medicines have yet to be fully discovered only for certain diseases. For ever so many other illnesses, medicines have been made ready.

You alone must sing the praises of this age’s excellence and glory.

We speak proudly of the holy age, the age of the *Bharata*. Yet King Dhritarashtra was blind even then. Which ascetic applied a medicinal leaf and cured his blindness? Had Dhritarashtra’s eyesight been sound, more than half the events of the *Bharata* would never have happened...

Ponni! This is not right, mind you! Why do you immediately pounce upon the Puranas whenever I say anything? Can you not sleep unless you raise that subject?

Leave your anger aside. Why did no one in those days give Dhritarashtra any medicine to restore his sight? Tell us. Let us hear.

Go and ask a preacher of the *Bharata*. He will give you two mighty slaps upon the cheek.

That is all, is it not? He will not answer the question? He cannot!

Reading and reading your party newspapers has made you excessively bold. You mock the Puranas and epics without the least fear. My husband told me about a man who works with him at the office. He had apparently been to Java. Do you know what he saw there?

I have heard that sugar is plentiful in Java.

Enough of your mockery and teasing, Ponni. In Java they apparently have our *Ramayana* and *Bharata* and all the rest! They have plays—a Rama play! They perform the story of the *Ramayana* as a dance...

### Image 8

Do you mean that? I knew it already. The books written by travellers contain page after page about it. They have even included pictures.

Ponni! Such sacred stories have spread throughout the world and been accepted by everyone. I say it is sheer wickedness for you always to condemn and mock them...

Say so! You call it only wickedness. I have heard many people who cannot contain their fury utter curses such as, “Your mouth will fester; your tongue will rot and fall out.”

So you are obstinate that, whatever anyone may say, you alone will never change your course... Hmph! Is that it?

Your party calls speaking in condemnation “obstinacy.” Our party considers it steadfastness to speak in defence of the truth.

Pick up every party and throw it into the rubbish bin...

Is that so! That is the programme of Jayaprakash Narayan’s party! There need be no parties at all. Parties competing with one another, seizing power and governing open the way to many evils. Jayaprakash Narayan therefore keeps saying there should be no parties—for which purpose there is a separate party!

Ponni! Let a thousand parties exist and be damned—but must there be a party whose sole work is condemning the Puranas and epics? Think it over and tell me... Speak without haste or anger...

When I think it over, I too feel there ought not to be a party for this...

Now you are talking... You have come to the right path...

### Image 9

Yes! If there is one party devoted to this, the other parties gird themselves and rise to fight it. So if all parties engage in exposing the fabrications in the Puranas, instead of turning this task into a party question, reason will gain dominion.
